right-hand-side of the FD.

Now we use the first item of the inductive
invariant on the fact
R_{\mathsf{mt}}(a_{1}\ldots a_{m})
: there was already a fact
F^{\prime\prime}
, either an
R
or

R^{\prime}
-fact, with tuple of values
(a_{1}\ldots a_{m},b^{\prime\prime}_{1}\ldots b^{\prime\prime}_{m})
.
As there is
1\leq i\leq m
such that
a^{\prime}_{i}\neq a_{i}
, the tuples of values
of
F^{\prime}
and
F^{\prime\prime}
must be different.
But now, as
F
and
F^{\prime}
are an FD violation on the positions

a_{1}\ldots a_{m}
, then
F^{\prime}
and
F^{\prime\prime}
are seen to also witness an FD
violation in
R\cup R^{\prime}
that existed before the firing. This
contradicts the first point of the invariant, so we conclude that the second
item is preserved when firing an
R_{\mathsf{mt}}
-to-
R
axiom.

When firing
R^{\prime}_{\mathsf{mt}}
-to-
R^{\prime}
rules, the symmetric argument applies.

This completes the proof of the invariant, and concludes the proof of
Theorem
47
.

D.7.
Choice Simplifiability for RA plans with
UID
s and FDs

We last turn to the adaptation of our choice simplifiability result for

UID
s and FDs (Theorem
26
). Here is the statement
for the case of RA plans:

Theorem 48
.

Let schema
{\mathsf{Sch}}
have constraints given by

UID
s and arbitrary FDs, and
Q
be a CQ that is access-determined w.r.t.
{\mathsf{Sch}}
.
Then
Q
is also access-determined in the choice simplification of
{\mathsf{Sch}}
.

We will proceed in a similar fashion to Theorem
26
,
i.e., fixing one access at a time. Here is the analogue of the single-access
blowup (Definition
7
), where we simply replace the access-valid
subinstance by a jointly access-valid subinstance:

{defi}

Let
{\mathsf{Sch}}
be a schema and
{\mathsf{Sch}}^{\dagger}
be its choice simplification,
and let
\Sigma
be a set of constraints.

Consider two instances
I_{1}^{\dagger},I_{2}^{\dagger}
that satisfy
\Sigma
, and a common subinstance

I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}^{\dagger}
which is jointly access-valid in
I_{1}^{\dagger}
and
I_{2}^{\dagger}

for
{\mathsf{Sch}}^{\dagger}
.
Let
({\mathsf{mt}},{\mathsf{AccBind}})
be an access in
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}^{\dagger}

A
single-access RA blowup
of
I_{1}^{\dagger},I_{2}^{\dagger}

and
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}^{\dagger}
for
({\mathsf{mt}},{\mathsf{AccBind}})
is a pair of
instances
I_{1},I_{2}
that
satisfy
\Sigma
,
such that
I_{1}
is a superinstance of
I_{1}^{\dagger}
,

I_{2}
has a homomorphism to
I_{2}^{\dagger}
,

I_{1}
and
I_{2}
have a common subinstance
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}
which is
jointly access-valid in
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
for
{\mathsf{Sch}}^{\dagger}
,
and the following hold:

(1)

I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}
is a superinstance of
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}^{\dagger}
;

(2)

there is an output to the access
{\mathsf{mt}},{\mathsf{AccBind}}
in
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}
which is valid
in
I_{1}

for
{\mathsf{Sch}}
;

(3)

for any access in
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}^{\dagger}
having an output
in
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}^{\dagger}
which is valid
for
{\mathsf{Sch}}
in
I_{1}^{\dagger}
, there is an output to this access
in
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}

which is valid for
{\mathsf{Sch}}
in
I_{1}
;

(4)

for any
access in
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}
which is not an access in
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}^{\dagger}
, there is an
output in
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}
which is valid for
{\mathsf{Sch}}
in
I_{1}
;

We use the following blowup lemma as an analogue of
Lemma
27
:

Lemma 49
.

Let
{\mathsf{Sch}}
be a schema and
{\mathsf{Sch}}^{\dagger}
be its choice simplification,
and let
\Sigma
be the set of constraints.

Assume that, for any CQ
Q
not access-determined in
{\mathsf{Sch}}^{\dagger}
, for any
counterexample
I_{1}^{\dagger},I_{2}^{\dagger}
of access-determinacy for
Q
and

{\mathsf{Sch}}^{\dagger}
with a common subinstance
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}^{\dagger}

jointly access-valid in
I_{1}^{\dagger}
and
I_{2}^{\dagger}
for
{\mathsf{Sch}}^{\dagger}
,
for any access
{\mathsf{mt}},{\mathsf{AccBind}}
in
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}^{\dagger}
,,
we can construct a single-access RA blowup of
I_{1}^{\dagger},I_{2}^{\dagger}

and
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}^{\dagger}

for
({\mathsf{mt}},{\mathsf{AccBind}})
.

Then any CQ which is access-determined in
{\mathsf{Sch}}
is also
access-determined in
{\mathsf{Sch}}^{\dagger}
.

The proof of this lemma is exactly like that of Lemma
27
.

We are now ready to prove Theorem
48
using the process
of Lemma
49
. We proceed similarly to the proof of
Theorem
26
.

Proof D.10
.

Let
{\mathsf{Sch}}
be the schema, let
{\mathsf{Sch}}^{\dagger}
be its choice
simplification, and let
\Sigma
be the set of constraints.
Let
Q
be a CQ which is not access-determined in
{\mathsf{Sch}}^{\dagger}
,
let
I_{1}^{\dagger},I_{2}^{\dagger}
be a counterexample to access-determinacy,
and let
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}^{\dagger}
be a common subinstance of
I_{1}^{\dagger}
and
I_{2}^{\dagger}

for
{\mathsf{Sch}}^{\dagger}

which is jointly access-valid in
I_{1}^{\dagger}
and
I_{2}^{\dagger}
for
{\mathsf{Sch}}^{\dagger}
.
Let
({\mathsf{mt}},{\mathsf{AccBind}})
be
an access on relation
R
in
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}^{\dagger}
:
we know that this access has an output which is valid for
{\mathsf{Sch}}^{\dagger}
, but it
does not necessarily have one
which is valid for
{\mathsf{Sch}}
.
Our proof is to follow the single-access RA blowup process and build
superinstances
I_{1}
,
I_{2}
, and
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}
of
I_{1}^{\dagger}
,
I_{2}^{\dagger}
, and

I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}^{\dagger}
respectively, which satisfy the conditions.

As in the proof of
Theorem
48
, if there are no matching tuples
in
I_{1}^{\dagger}

for the access
({\mathsf{mt}},{\mathsf{AccBind}})
, then there are no matching tuples in
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}^{\dagger}
either,
so the access
({\mathsf{mt}},{\mathsf{AccBind}})
already has a valid output for
{\mathsf{Sch}}
and there
is nothing to do. The same holds if there are no matching tuples in
I_{2}^{\dagger}
.
Now, if there is exactly one matching tuple in
I_{1}^{\dagger}
and exactly one matching
tuple in
I_{2}^{\dagger}
, as
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}^{\dagger}
is jointly access-valid for
{\mathsf{Sch}}^{\dagger}
, it
necessarily contains these matching tuples, so that, as
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}^{\dagger}\subseteq I_{1}^{\dagger}

and
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}^{\dagger}\subseteq I_{2}^{\dagger}
, the matching tuple in
I_{1}^{\dagger}

and
I_{2}^{\dagger}
is the same,
and again there is nothing to do: the access
({\mathsf{mt}},{\mathsf{AccBind}})
already has a
valid output for
{\mathsf{Sch}}
.

Hence, the only interesting case is when there is a matching tuple to the access
in
I_{1}^{\dagger}
and in
I_{2}^{\dagger}
, and there is more than one matching tuple in one of the
two. As
I_{1}^{\dagger}
and
I_{2}^{\dagger}
play a symmetric role in the hypotheses of
Lemma
49
, we assume without loss of generality that it is

I_{1}^{\dagger}
which has multiple matching tuples for the access.

As
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}^{\dagger}
is access-valid in
I_{1}^{\dagger}
for
{\mathsf{Sch}}^{\dagger}
, we know
that
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}^{\dagger}
contains at least one of these tuples, say
\vec{t}_{1}
. As
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}\subseteq I_{2}^{\dagger}
, then
I_{2}^{\dagger}
also contains
\vec{t}_{1}
. As in the
proof of Theorem
26
,
we take
\vec{t}_{2}
a different matching tuple in
I_{1}^{\dagger}
, let
C
be the non-empty
set of positions where
\vec{t}_{1}
and
\vec{t}_{2}
disagree, and observe that
there is no FD implied from the complement of
C
to a position of
C
.

We define
W
as in the proof of
Theorem
26
,
and construct
I_{1}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}I_{1}^{\dagger}\cup W
and
I_{2}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}I_{2}^{\dagger}\cup W
as in that proof. We
show that
(I_{1},I_{2})
is a counterexample to determinacy for
Q

and
{\mathsf{Sch}}^{\dagger}
:

•

We know by Claim
7.3
that
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
satisfy the
UID
s and the
FDs of
\Sigma
.

•

We clearly have
I_{1}^{\dagger}\subseteq I_{1}
.

•

The homomorphism
from
I_{2}
to
I_{2}^{\dagger}
is
defined as in the proof of Theorem
26
.

•

We define
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}^{\dagger}\cup W

a common subinstance of
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
and we
must show that
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}
is
jointly access-valid in
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
for
{\mathsf{Sch}}^{\dagger}
.
We do this as in the
proof of Theorem
26
.
First, for accesses that include an element of
{\mathsf{Adom}}(I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}})\setminus{\mathsf{Adom}}(I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}^{\dagger})
, the matching tuples are all in
W
so they are
in
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}
.
Second,
for accesses on
{\mathsf{Adom}}(I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}^{\dagger})
, the matching tuples include the
result
U
of this access in
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}^{\dagger}
, which was valid in
I_{1}^{\dagger}
and

I_{2}^{\dagger}
, and possible additional matching tuples
U^{\prime}
from
W
which are
in
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}
, and these are the only possible matching tuples.
Thus, we can construct a valid output
to this access for
{\mathsf{Sch}}^{\dagger}
from
U
and
U^{\prime}
.

What remains to be able to use Lemma
49
is to show the
four additional conditions:

(1)

It is immediate that
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}\supseteq I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}^{\dagger}
.

(2)

The access
({\mathsf{mt}},{\mathsf{AccBind}})
has an output in
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}
which is valid for
{\mathsf{Sch}}

in
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
. This is established as in the proof of
Theorem
26
: there are now
infinitely many matching tuples for the access in
I_{1}

and
I_{2}
, so we can choose
as many as we want in
W
to obtain an output in
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}
which is valid
for
{\mathsf{Sch}}
in
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
.

(3)

For every access of
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}^{\dagger}
that has an output which is valid
for
{\mathsf{Sch}}
in
I_{1}^{\dagger}

in
I_{2}^{\dagger}
, then we can construct such an output in
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}
which is
valid for
{\mathsf{Sch}}
in
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
.
This is similar to the fourth bullet point above. From the
output
U
to the access in
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}^{\dagger}
which is valid for
I_{1}^{\dagger}
and

I_{2}^{\dagger}
,
we construct an
output to the access in
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}
which is valid for
I_{1}

and
I_{2}
, using the tuples
of
U
and the matching tuples in
W
.

(4)

All accesses of
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}
which are not accesses
of
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}^{\dagger}
have an
output which is valid
for
{\mathsf{Sch}}
in
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
. As before, such accesses must include
an element of
W
, so by the fourth bullet point all matching tuples are
in
W
, so they are all in
I_{\mathsf{\scriptscriptstyle Accessed}}
.

Hence, we have explained how to fix the access
({\mathsf{mt}},{\mathsf{AccBind}})
, so we can
conclude using Lemma
49
that we obtain a counterexample to
access-determinacy of
Q
in
{\mathsf{Sch}}
by fixing all accesses. This concludes
the proof.

D.8.
Summary of Extensions to Answerability with RA plans

Table
2
summarizes the expressiveness and complexity results
for RA plans. There are two differences with the corresponding table for
monotone answerability (Table
1
in the body):

•

For RA plans, while we know that choice simplifiability holds with FDs and
UIDs, we do not know whether answerability is decidable. Indeed, in the
monotone case, when proving Theorem
29
, we had used a separability argument
to show that FDs could be ignored for FDs and UIDs (see the
proof of Theorem
29
in Section
8
).
We do not have such an
argument for answerability with RA plans.

•

For RA plans, our tight complexity bound for answerability with FDs in
isolation holds only under the
{\mathsf{SMPR}}
assumption; see
Appendix
D.6
for
details.
